You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer array nums and a positive integer k. Return the sum of the maximum and minimum elements of all subarrays with at most k elements.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [1,2,3], k = 2
Output: 20
Explanation:
The subarrays of nums with at most 2 elements are:

The output would be -6.

 
Constraints:

- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

prev_ge = [-1]*n
stack=[]
for i,x in enumerate(nums):
    while stack and nums[stack[-1]] < x:
        stack.pop()
    prev_ge[i] = stack[-1] if stack else -1
    stack.append(i)

next_gt = [n]*n
stack=[]
for i in range(n-1,-1,-1):
    x=nums[i]
    while stack and nums[stack[-1]] <= x:
        stack.pop()
    next_gt[i] = stack[-1] if stack else n
    stack.append(i)

prev_le = [-1]*n
stack=[]
for i,x in enumerate(nums):
    while stack and nums[stack[-1]] > x:
        stack.pop()
    prev_le[i] = stack[-1] if stack else -1
    stack.append(i)

next_lt = [n]*n
stack=[]
for i in range(n-1,-1,-1):
    x=nums[i]
    while stack and nums[stack[-1]] >= x:
        stack.pop()
    next_lt[i] = stack[-1] if stack else n
    stack.append(i)

Then compute ans = sum over i:
K = k-1
a = i - prev_ge[i]
b = next_gt[i] - i
cnt = count(a,b,K)
ans += nums[i]*cnt
a = i - prev_le[i]
b = next_lt[i] - i
cnt = count(a,b,K)
ans += nums[i]*cnt
Return ans.

Let's write code:

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minMaxSubarraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        n = len(nums)
        K = k - 1

        def tri(m: int) -> int:
            if m < 0:
                return 0
            return (m + 1) * (m + 2) // 2

        def count_pairs(a: int, b: int, T: int) -> int:
            if a <= 0 or b <= 0 or T < 0:
                return 0
            return tri(T) - tri(T - a) - tri(T - b) + tri(T - a - b)

        prev_ge = [-1] * n
        prev_le = [-1] * n
        st_ge = []
        st_le = []
        for i, x in enumerate(nums):
            while st_ge and nums[st_ge[-1]] < x:
                st_ge.pop()
            if st_ge:
                prev_ge[i] = st_ge[-1]
            st_ge.append(i)

            while st_le and nums[st_le[-1]] > x:
                st_le.pop()
            if st_le:
                prev_le[i] = st_le[-1]
            st_le.append(i)

        next_gt = [n] * n
        next_lt = [n] * n
        st_gt = []
        st_lt = []
        for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            x = nums[i]
            while st_gt and nums[st_gt[-1]] <= x:
                st_gt.pop()
            if st_gt:
                next_gt[i] = st_gt[-1]
            st_gt.append(i)

            while st_lt and nums[st_lt[-1]] >= x:
                st_lt.pop()
            if st_lt:
                next_lt[i] = st_lt[-1]
            st_lt.append(i)

        ans = 0
        for i in range(n):
            a = i - prev_ge[i]
            b = next_gt[i] - i
            ans += nums[i] * count_pairs(a, b, K)

            a = i - prev_le[i]
            b = next_lt[i] - i
            ans += nums[i] * count_pairs(a, b, K)

        return ans

Before final, let's inspect code for any syntax issues.

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minMaxSubarraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        n = len(nums)
        K = k - 1

        def tri(m: int) -> int:
            if m < 0:
                return 0
            return (m + 1) * (m + 2) // 2

        def count_pairs(a: int, b: int, T: int) -> int:
            if a <= 0 or b <= 0 or T < 0:
                return 0
            return tri(T) - tri(T - a) - tri(T - b) + tri(T - a - b)

        prev_ge = [-1] * n
        prev_le = [-1] * n
        st_ge = []
        st_le = []

        for i, x in enumerate(nums):
            while st_ge and nums[st_ge[-1]] < x:
                st_ge.pop()
            if st_ge:
                prev_ge[i] = st_ge[-1]
            st_ge.append(i)

            while st_le and nums[st_le[-1]] > x:
                st_le.pop()
            if st_le:
                prev_le[i] = st_le[-1]
            st_le.append(i)

        next_gt = [n] * n
        next_lt = [n] * n
        st_gt = []
        st_lt = []

        for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            x = nums[i]
            while st_gt and nums[st_gt[-1]] <= x:
                st_gt.pop()
            if st_gt:
                next_gt[i] = st_gt[-1]
            st_gt.append(i)

            while st_lt and nums[st_lt[-1]] >= x:
                st_lt.pop()
            if st_lt:
                next_lt[i] = st_lt[-1]
            st_lt.append(i)

        ans = 0
        for i in range(n):
            a = i - prev_ge[i]
            b = next_gt[i] - i
            ans += nums[i] * count_pairs(a, b, K)

            a = i - prev_le[i]
            b = next_lt[i] - i
            ans += nums[i] * count_pairs(a, b, K)

        return ans
```

Looks good.

Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "minimum" for all equal [5,5], i
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minMaxSubarraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        n = len(nums)
        K = k - 1

        def tri(m: int) -> int:
            if m < 0:
                return 0
            return (m + 1) * (m + 2) // 2

        def count_pairs(a: int, b: int, T: int) -> int:
            if a <= 0 or b <= 0 or T < 0:
                return 0
            return tri(T) - tri(T - a) - tri(T - b) + tri(T - a - b)

        prev_ge = [-1] * n
        prev_le = [-1] * n
        st_ge = []
        st_le = []

        for i, x in enumerate(nums):
            while st_ge and nums[st_ge[-1]] < x:
                st_ge.pop()
            if st_ge:
                prev_ge[i] = st_ge[-1]
            st_ge.append(i)

            while st_le and nums[st_le[-1]] > x:
                st_le.pop()
            if st_le:
                prev_le[i] = st_le[-1]
            st_le.append(i)

        next_gt = [n] * n
        next_lt = [n] * n
        st_gt = []
        st_lt = []

        for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            x = nums[i]

            while st_gt and nums[st_gt[-1]] <= x:
                st_gt.pop()
            if st_gt:
                next_gt[i] = st_gt[-1]
            st_gt.append(i)

            while st_lt and nums[st_lt[-1]] >= x:
                st_lt.pop()
            if st_lt:
                next_lt[i] = st_lt[-1]
            st_lt.append(i)

        ans = 0

        for i in range(n):
            a = i - prev_ge[i]
            b = next_gt[i] - i
            ans += nums[i] * count_pairs(a, b, K)

            a = i - prev_le[i]
            b = next_lt[i] - i
            ans += nums[i] * count_pairs(a, b, K)

        return ans
```
